## November 12, 2026, 2:41 PM: ch 26, "Your thumbs were in" (CLOSED: option A applied 2:46 PM)

Todd selected the line and asked: "what does this mean". Answer given: she is telling him he caught her correctly. Fifteen lines up he stands with his thumbs tucked against his fingers so a falling climber does not bend them back, and the catch says "thumbs in". She felt it and names the technique where someone else would say thank you. It leans on a setup the reader may not be holding, and "in" does not say in what.

- Option 1: keep the line.
- Option 2: “You knew where to put your hands,” she said.
- Option 3: “You tucked your thumbs,” she said. “Who showed you that?” / “Nobody. I read it in 2008.” (and "thumbs in" in the catch becomes "thumbs tucked" to match)
- My pick: 3. She assumes a person taught him, because that is how she learned everything; he learned it alone from a page. It pays off "I had read how to spot a climber in 2008 and had never done it, because I climbed alone."
- Todd, 2:44 PM: "3 doesnt make sense. Nor does 2." He is right, and the reason reaches the original line too: she has never climbed, so she has no reason to know or care where a spotter's thumbs go. The technique is his business. What happened to her is that someone held her weight. Second round:
  - Option A: “You had me,” she said. (what a mover says when the other person has the load; the reader hears the other meaning)
  - Option B: cut the line. She leans back for a second, steps off the pad, and goes straight back up the wall.
  - My pick: A.
- Todd, 2:46 PM: "A". Applied to the chapter file, the manuscript doc and the Vellum book. Rule from this: a character only remarks on what she would know or care about; Jo has never climbed.

- Checked (USGS, Hawaii County, Hawaii Supreme Court): Kamaʻehuakanaloa (Lōʻihi until 2021) is an active undersea volcano about 19 to 22 miles off the southeast coast, summit about 3,200 feet down, name glossed "reddish child of Kanaloa" (Kanaloa, the ocean), no known date for reaching the surface (USGS: as much as 200,000 years). The 2018 eruption made 875 acres of new land and destroyed 716 dwellings. Land made by lava belongs to the State (State ex rel. Kobayashi v. Zimring, 1977).

## Standing: what "reveal character" means (Todd, November 10, 1:18 PM)

- Todd, on the ch 4 garage bet: "This is what I mean by revealing character. Too often you just make up single lines that don't mean anything."
- The standard: when Todd asks for a line to reveal character, look first for an exchange or an action where two beliefs meet, with something at stake that nobody says out loud, and if possible a payoff later in the book. The bet works because Jo pushes (she doesn't believe him), Cal answers from his belief (he doesn't take chances), Jo reads him ("Smart"), the stake is an extra Saturday together, and ch 34 pays it off.
- A single invented line that only sounds like the character, or restates a fact the reader already has, doesn't meet it. Offer that kind only when the spot can't hold an exchange, and say so.

## November 11, 10:17 PM: ch 19, the lanai passage cut down so it hits (CURRENT TEXT)

- Todd: "Do you think when readers read it will hit hard for them? That's what I want". Claude's answer: the last paragraph hits; the middle (the sums, the three-trucks joke, "Asking first") cools the reader right before the blow. Shorter version offered; Todd: "yes".
- The passage now, three paragraphs: "It wasn't hard to find." / "Kevin's folks gave us a down payment. I didn't hang a picture for eleven years." / "I knew what this one would cost. For one minute I'd wanted it anyway, handcuffs and all. I'd wanted to put my whole weight on him, me and my kid. I hadn't known I was that tired."
- Out: "That was a down payment. This was all of it. The house, the college, my father's knees. I did the math on the lanai. Three trucks till I'm dead wouldn't touch it." and "So I'd live in it like I lived in that one. Asking first." The 10:14 PM entry below has the longer text and the reasoning.
- Todd did not object to the added "whole weight" sentence; it stays (plant for ch 35).
- Lesson: for a line meant to hit, get to the turn fast and let the reader do the arithmetic. A joke before the blow lets them off.
- In the manuscript doc, manuscript/chapter-19-jo.md, the Vellum book and the rebuilt Word file.

## November 11, 10:14 PM: ch 19, the lanai: what Jo is afraid of (her Pemberley moment)

- Todd, on "It wasn't hard to find. I could never pay him back for a house like that. All I could do was lean on him, me and my kid, with our whole weight.": "Is there something more insightful and deeper we can do here?" Four rounds were rejected before one landed. His notes, in order: option 1 "isn't fully developed nor does it stay in character"; the developed version "doesn't land or hit hard. It's go through events"; three short ones "aren't any different. I want you to think about it and try to think of something deep from her perspective. What is she afraid of? This is her pemberly moment from pride and prejudice. Why would she be afraid to have it all? And say it how she would say it." Then "ok".
- What it is now (four short paragraphs): "It wasn't hard to find." / "Kevin's folks gave us a down payment. I didn't hang a picture for eleven years. That was a down payment. This was all of it. The house, the college, my father's knees. I did the math on the lanai. Three trucks till I'm dead wouldn't touch it." / "So I'd live in it like I lived in that one. Asking first." / "I knew all that. For one minute I'd wanted it anyway, handcuffs and all. I'd wanted to put my whole weight on him, me and my kid. I hadn't known I was that tired."
- The fear, as settled with Todd: she has already lived in a house somebody else paid for (ch 17: the down payment, "To him it was a present. To me it was handcuffs."); this is that with no way to pay it back; and she wanted it anyway. Being afraid of how much she wanted it is the turn. Built on: "handcuffs" (ch 17), her "ask me first" rule turned around (ch 17), Owen's "She was tired." (ch 12).
- One sentence was added after Todd's "ok" and flagged to him: "I'd wanted to put my whole weight on him, me and my kid." It keeps the plant for ch 35 ("I put my whole weight down on him, all of it, on purpose. He took it."), which the approved draft would have dropped.
- What failed, to avoid next time: Claude explaining her ("I never picked anything up"); Jo summing up; walking back through events already on the page; three variants of the same idea offered as different. When Todd asks for depth, find the fear in what she has already lived on the page and give one version.
- In the manuscript doc, manuscript/chapter-19-jo.md, the Vellum book and the rebuilt Word file.

Wine: “It is as good as it was on the deck,” he said. “Most of the bottles in my cellar were better at the winery.” (the cellar is Claude's). The lean: "That fire was mostly for looks. The warm side of me was the one next to Cal. I had a sweatshirt in the cab. I didn't go get it. I slid down the bench until my shoulder was against his. He held still and let me lean. I leaned on him more than the guest room called for." (wood throws more radiant heat than gas; "A gas fire warms your knees and nothing else" was untrue).

Then the whole canyon was full of them. The hair stood up on my arms." / “How many is that?” / “Two,” Cal said. “A pair, most likely. They sound like ten.” / “Two's plenty.” / "They quit all at once. Pancake hadn't even lifted his head."
- Bats (they skim to drink at dusk while the sky is light, one at a time along the same lane): "Bats came out over the pool while the water still had the last of the sky in it. They came down one at a time on the same line, touched the water and went back up, like my guys taking turns on a ramp. I'd never seen a bat drink."
- Hot tub (Todd: "nobody goes feet or knees into a hot tub. They go naked."; then the pool house was his idea): “There are some in the pool house.” The pool house is set up like a spa (two bathrooms, a steam room, showers inside and out, a stone counter with two sinks and a blow dryer at each), one wall of oiled teak ("On a job I pad teak twice."), towels rolled in the cubbies, swimsuits folded by size with the tags on. "I wasn't going to stand in his pool house holding swimsuits up against myself, looking for my size. I took a towel." She comes out in the towel, clothes folded in thirds and then in half; "Somebody was paying PG&E to keep that hot for nobody."; “Turn around,” he looks up into the oaks with his hands at his sides; “Okay,”; "I watched him do it. Nobody had told me not to." The line about Darlene's towels with tags was cut (Todd: "cut").
- In the water: his toast, Janice's (“To a lovely evening, and to the company that made it one.” / “That's Janice's.” / “She wrote it for my board dinners.” / “Say yours.” / “To the guest room.”), then the foot, then “Nobody's seen me naked in three years.” / “I still have not. No Milky Way: in 19 she has never seen it. He watches her, not the sky. "We stayed like that… until my fingers had gone to prunes." ("until the fire was down to the blue" was wrong for gas.) "In that water he'd have put it down to the heat."

I come home after dark.” / “I have not come home to it dark since I bought it.” / “Mine's dark half the week… I turn the radio on in the kitchen so somebody's talking.”); Cal lifts Pancake up the three back steps (ch 2's "every night"); “May I ask you something?” / “Why the guest room?” / "He'd let me have it with no reason since Sunday." / “Ask me at my house.” / “When?” / “Saturday.” This is where 18's Saturday comes from (he texts Thursday asking what time).

- Fri 8:43 PM Remy's name (Todd's backstory, worked out 8:31–8:42): Cal's father was Jeremy; he left in 1990 when Cal was five, no reason ever given; Darlene's first silence was that week. That fall she brought a dog home from the county shelter and let Cal name him; he named him Remy. Brown, part beagle; waited at the front door every day at 3:10; slept on Cal's feet; was who Cal talked to in the nine days; died when Cal was twelve (1997). Darlene called him in every night for seven years and never asked why. Cal has had a dog ever since. Cover story: “It is short and easy to spell”; a reporter guessed Remington (the street) in 2014 and Cal never corrected him (not a lie). Cal had not said “Jeremy” out loud since he was five.

## October 21, 5:08 PM: the struck line opens its chapter; kept

- 5:03 PM, Todd sent Chapter 1 in Vellum: "1. 5′8″ or taller" struck, in the written-note font (now an all-caps marker face, not the script of his 4:27 PM card screenshot), and the drop cap on "Dawn". Kept.
- Final form: in the chapter files the struck line is its own paragraph right under the narrator line (`**Jo**`, blank, `~~1. 5'8″ or taller~~`, blank, first paragraph); the doc has the same paragraph, struck, in all eleven chapters (checked by hash); the project has the eleven files. `claude/build-vellum-docx.js` sets that paragraph as a Vellum Written Note; the Word file came out identical to the one Todd imported at 5:03, so it was not rewritten. Rule for any new chapter: when Jo strikes a line in it, it opens with that line, the printout's wording and number.
- Asked Todd to check that the cards (1, 36, epilogue) still read well in the new note font, since Vellum uses one font for every note.
- Todd, 5:10 PM: "I created a separate note variant for the card so it works." Vellum keeps a note's variant in the .vellum file (styleVariantNumber), not in the Word import, so after any fresh import of the Word file the three cards (ch 1 Janice's, ch 36 Owen's, the epilogue's) must be set to the card variant again by hand; the eleven struck lines stay on the default note style.

I would like to ask it now." / "May I pay off your house?"), and she holds the counter, shuts her mouth on the no and lets him get to the end of it: "Yes."; his eyes fill and he does not say it was the horseradish; "I am sorry." with nothing after it, and she waits for the rest and there is none; she sends him for water ("Glasses are by the sink"), he opens the cupboard on the right, finds her tin, holds it shut in both hands and puts it back square, and does not ask; two bowls ("Caldo verde." "I have never had it." "My father's. He makes it when it rains."); the refund check goes into the white truck ("I'm ordering the part in the morning, and the shop gets the rest on the first"; "My dad's been in that cab since July." / "Then he is off the truck."), which is what lets him tell her seven ("I wanted someone who would use what I sent. Then I would not have to come. I could pick it from the back of a car and never be in the room." / "And when she doesn't?" / "I did not have that answer in June." / "Now?" / "I am in your kitchen."); she writes seven under GOOD FAMILY with the carpenter's pencil from the drawer by the stove and strikes it, two left; Owen's video and his one-character answer, a 1; "He sleeps better on his own bed. I will carry him out at nine." / "I need you to stay." / "Both of you. Tonight. And the next time something breaks."; his watch face down in the clear spot by the coffee maker where the statement used to live; his hand on the back of her neck under the chain; "He goes out at five." "Then we go out at five."; the third love scene, open door and short (she does not put him anywhere; he does not present; "I got him out of his jeans and got on top of him and took him in... I put my whole weight down on him, all of it, on purpose. He took it." / "I set the pace. He kept to it... Then his hands tightened on my hips and he quit waiting. I didn't want him to wait."); the dog across her bedroom door; the third "I love you" with his heart going under her palm, and she does not make him wait ("I love you. Mine's doing the same thing."); the scene ends on the dog's breath; at four minutes to five he is up to walk the dog alone and she says "I said we."; in his fleece, down the middle of her street in the fog, the dog at the corner signpost, she holds out her hand for the leash and he puts the loop in her palm; last line: "Cal walked up the middle of the street beside me with nothing in his hands."

Bring Pancake." instead of "Nothing"; Cal says "I am sorry" with nothing after it (the sentence his engine could not say, placed here); the refund check pays for the white truck's transmission (the open object from 33); the chapter ends on her street at five on Monday, not in bed.

## Old Standing section (superseded by claude/standing.md on November 12; kept for the doc ids and tool recipes, not as rules)

### Read this first

- The book: a Kindle Unlimited romance, "Nobody Marries the List," pen name Wendy Garrity. First person, past tense, alternating Jo (odd chapters) and Cal (even chapters). 36 chapters and an epilogue, heat 3 of 5.
- Where it stands: chapters 1 to 36 and the epilogue are drafted (October 20).
- Before drafting anything, read the plan's entry for the chapter, the style guide and the character sheet, and the two chapters before it. Run Todd's /fiction-craft skill. No agents (October 20). Write proposals to a project file before asking Todd, so they outlive the session (October 17 lesson).
- Outside critiques: give an opinion first and change nothing until he says so. His own line notes: fix them and show the changed lines. When Todd asks what a line means, answer, propose the exact line, and wait for his yes.

### Where everything lives

Project files (the source of truth):
- `manuscript/chapter-01-jo.md` to `manuscript/chapter-36-cal.md`: one file per chapter, current.
- `claude/handoff-current.md` (this file) and `claude/handoff.md` (the archive).
- `claude/pending-updates.md`: changes the plan file and the character sheet file still need (empty as of October 20, 11:00 AM; record only).
- `claude/book-plan.md`: the plan, chapter by chapter, with the calendar and chronology. Over 480 KB; `project_read` truncates it, so edit the plan doc in place and leave the file change in pending-updates.md.
- `claude/romance-style-guide.md`: genre rules, Todd's sentence rules, and the list of things to keep out.
- `claude/character-sheet.md`: voices, engines, facts, and who knows what.
- `claude/build-vellum-docx.js`: builds the Vellum-ready Word file from the chapter files (`NODE_PATH=$(npm root -g) node build-vellum-docx.js <folder> <output.docx>`). Todd, October 8: "Don't worry about vellum file." Do not offer a rebuild.
- `claude/research-notes.md`, `claude/proposals-dawn.md`, `claude/review-narration-in-dialogue.md`: records.

Writing to the Vellum book is now allowed, through the official Vellum plugin's tools (Todd, November 4, 12:52 PM: "Change my standing order now that we have an official vellum plugin"; replaces "Never write to a Vellum file"). Nothing has been committed from here; he checks it in himself.

### How Todd wants the work done

- Interesting people saying interesting things; no generic lines (November 2). Research anything outside common knowledge before offering it, and say what was checked (November 2). When a line refers to Pancake, it says Pancake (November 2). Hold disk files while he is making changes (November 2).
- The genre's style governs. It must read like a romance novel at all times. Sentence rules apply: no em dashes, none of the listed AI-sounding phrases, short paragraphs whose first sentences carry the story.
- Every line does work; everything from the character's perspective; think it through, no patches (October 6). Everything right, not just what he can check (October 7).
- Say the thing; do not be coy. No character speaks narration (October 18). Nothing from an earlier chapter restated unless it does new work (October 19). Clock times are Cal's (October 17). Places at full size (October 15). Dawn may say true things about both of them; nobody else explains him to himself.
- A revised chapter replaces the old one. Never keep superseded chapters. Scene breaks are a line holding only `***` (in the doc, a plain paragraph of three asterisks, written as `\*\*\*` in markdown).
- Always show the link to the full manuscript in replies, not chapter files ("The chapters don't refresh"). After every manuscript change, reopen the manuscript doc for him with the Artifact tool's open action.
- The Word file is rebuilt only when Todd asks (November 12), not after each change. When rebuilt it is always ~/NobodyMarriesTheList/Nobody Marries the List.docx, overwritten in place, never renamed (Todd, October 20, 4:26 PM: "It needs to always have the same name so I can reimport it into vellum.").
- Every doc has one header line with the full date and time of the most recent update; overwrite it on every change, with the real time (check the clock first).
- Keep moving and keep him posted; a long silent stretch drew "Are you spinning."

### Tone with Todd

- He sends short notes, often a quoted line and a few words. Take the note seriously, find the real cause, fix it, say what changed, and quote the new lines so he can see them without opening anything.
- Do not explain or defend at length. Do not add things he did not ask for without flagging them.
- When he asks a question, answer it before changing anything.

### Working notes (tools)

- Project files are read whole with the Projects tool and written back whole (`project_write` with `local_path`). Subagents cannot read the project: put what they need on local disk first (this session used `nmtl/manuscript/`, `nmtl/claude/` and `nmtl/review/`).
- Docs: a search read (`{"kind":"search","text":...}`) finds a paragraph; `replace` with a find target and `"as":"text"` changes words across runs (that is how the header time is changed); a whole paragraph is replaced by block id with `ifHash` and `ifRev`; a new chapter goes in with an `insert` at `{"kind":"root"}`, `"side":"end"`, as markdown. An in-page link cannot ride a span-grade replace: to rebuild the Jump-to row, insert a new markdown paragraph after the old row and delete the old block with its guards, in one call.
- No agents (Todd, October 20). Every review is done in the main session. The earlier practice of a separate cold reviewer is retired.
